There's no single built-in skill that handles the full workflow (conflict resolution + review response + auto-merge in bulk). Here's what exists and what's closest to what you need:

**Closest existing options:**

- **`/schedule`** — Creates a cron-scheduled remote agent. You could set up an agent that runs every N minutes, checks your open PRs via `gh` CLI or the GitHub MCP tools, resolves update-branch conflicts, and merges when green. This is probably the best fit for "bulk, automated, recurring."

- **`/loop`** — Runs a prompt on a repeating interval in the current session. Simpler, but session-scoped (stops when you close Claude Code).

- **`superpowers:finishing-a-development-branch`** — Guides the merge decision workflow, but it's per-branch and manual, not bulk.

**What you'd realistically need to build:**

A scheduled agent prompt that does:
1. `gh pr list --state open` — find candidate PRs
2. `gh pr checks` — confirm CI is green
3. `gh pr update-branch` — rebase if behind main
4. Wait for CI re-run, then `gh pr merge --squash`
5. Handle CodeRabbit review comments before merging

> AGENT

Yes — scheduled agents run **entirely in Anthropic's cloud**, not on your machine. From the skill:

> "NOT local cron jobs — each trigger spawns a fully isolated remote session (CCR) in Anthropic's cloud infrastructure on a cron schedule."

So:
- Your laptop can be off, closed, or idle — the agent still fires
- It gets its own git checkout of your repo in a sandboxed environment
- It has no access to your local files or environment — the prompt needs to be self-contained

The only caveat for your PR merge use case: it would need the **Claude GitHub App** installed on your repo (`ravencloak-org/Raven`) to be able to interact with PRs. That's a one-time setup at `https://claude.ai/code/onboarding?magic=github-app-setup`.

Want me to set one up now that periodically checks your open PRs, resolves conflicts, handles CodeRabbit feedback, and squash-merges when green?

> DEVELOPER

Yeah, do you think that makes sense? Is that what people do when they have a lot of pull requests to close, which keep getting added every hour or two?

> AGENT

Honestly, a Claude scheduled agent is probably overkill for the core problem. Here's what people actually use:

**For "merge when checks pass" (simplest case):**
```bash
gh pr merge --auto --squash
```
Enable auto-merge per PR when you create it — GitHub merges it automatically the moment CI goes green. No agent needed.

**For "keep branch up to date with main" (conflict prevention):**
GitHub has a built-in setting: **"Always suggest updating pull request branches"** + **auto-merge on update**. Or tools like **Mergify** handle this declaratively with rules like:
```yaml
- name: auto-merge when green
  conditions: [check-success=CI, -conflict]
  actions:
    merge: {method: squash}
```

**For "rebase conflicting PRs automatically":**
Mergify also handles this — it queues PRs, rebases them one at a time against main, runs CI, merges. This is what high-volume teams (50+ PRs/day) actually use.

**Where a Claude agent actually adds value** that the above can't:
- Reading CodeRabbit feedback and deciding if it's a blocker vs. nitpick
- Writing responses to review comments or making minor fixes
- Cross-PR dependency ordering

**My recommendation for your situation:**
1. Enable `--auto` on PRs when your worktree agents create them
2. Set up Mergify for conflict rebasing + squash merge rules
3. Only use a Claude agent for the CodeRabbit review-response part if that's actually blocking you

Perfect! Now I have all the information needed. Let me compile a comprehensive report:

## Report: Pull Request Creation Analysis for Raven Repository

Based on my thorough investigation of the repository at `/Users/jobinlawrance/Project/raven`, here are my findings:

---

### 1. **Branch Naming Pattern**

The repository uses a **consistent semantic branch naming convention**:

- **Feature branches**: `feat/stream-backend-m4`, `feat/issue-23-jwt-middleware`, `feat/cloudflare-pages-deployment`
- **Fix branches**: `fix/ci-failures`, `fix/ci-failures-m2`, `fix/dockerfile-and-coderabbit`
- **Refactor branches**: `refactor/python-utility-libs`
- **Dependency branches**: `deps/bump-actions`, `deps/bump-typescript`
- **CI/Chore branches**: `ci/playwright-mypy-workflows`, `ci/pr-agent`, `chore/badges-and-codecov`
- **Legacy milestone branches**: `feature/1.1-go-module`, `feature/1.2-python-worker`, `feature/1.3-vue-frontend` (early phase)

**Pattern**: `<type>/<descriptor>` where type is `feat`, `fix`, `refactor`, `deps`, `ci`, `chore`, etc.

---

### 2. **Auto-Merge Flag Usage**

**No `--auto` flag is being used.** The repository does not employ automated PR merging. Instead:

- PRs are created manually through **Claude Code agent workflow** or **GitHub API calls**
- The `.entire/settings.json` is configured with `"strategy": "manual-commit"` (not auto-merge)
- No Mergify configuration or auto-merge GitHub Actions are present
- PRs appear to wait for manual review and approval before merging

---

### 3. **Mergify Configuration**

**Mergify does NOT exist.** There is:
- No `.mergify.yml` or `.mergify.yaml` file
- No Mergify CI checks configured
- No automated merge rules

---

### 4. **CI Checks Running on PRs**

All PRs to `main` branch trigger **8 parallel CI workflows** based on path filters:

| Workflow | Triggers On | Checks |
|----------|-------------|--------|
| **Go CI** | Go code changes (`cmd/`, `internal/`, `pkg/`, `go.mod`, `go.sum`) | Build, test (-race), migration test, coverage upload (Codecov), vet, golangci-lint |
| **Python CI** | Python code changes (`ai-worker/`, `proto/`) | Ruff lint, ruff format check, mypy type checking, pytest with coverage |
| **Frontend CI** | Frontend changes (`frontend/`, workflow file) | Lint, TypeScript type check (vue-tsc), build, **Playwright E2E tests** |
| **Docker CI** | Docker changes (`Dockerfile`, `ai-worker/Dockerfile`, `docker-compose.yml`) | Multi-arch Docker builds (go-api, python-worker) |
| **Pages CI** | Pages-related changes | Build & deploy to Cloudflare Pages |
| **Security Scanning** | Weekly cron + push/PR | Trivy vulnerability scanner, Go vulnerability checker |
| **Claude Code** | Comments mentioning `@claude` | Runs Claude Code action for AI-assisted reviews |
| **OpenCode** | PR review comments | Runs OpenCode analysis |

**Status Checks Required**: While no explicit branch protection rule is visible in the config files, the multiple CI jobs act as de facto status checks for PR mergeability.

---

### 5. **Where the Bottleneck Likely Is**

Based on the git history and CI setup, the bottleneck is **multi-faceted**:

1. **Frontend E2E Tests (~5-10 min)**: The Playwright E2E tests in the Frontend CI job are the slowest CI check. They:
   - Install dependencies
   - Build the frontend
   - Install Playwright browsers
   - Run full browser automation tests
   
   This is significantly slower than Go/Python/Docker builds.

2. **Multiple Parallel Workflows**: While workflows run in parallel, any failure in one blocks the PR:
   - If Frontend E2E fails, the entire PR is blocked
   - If Go tests fail (with `-race` flag), it blocks merging
   - Coverage and linting failures also block

3. **Manual Review Requirement**: The `.entire/settings.json` uses `"strategy": "manual-commit"`, indicating:
   - Claude Code agents cannot auto-merge PRs
   - All PRs require explicit human review and approval
   - No automated merging even after CI passes

4. **No Branch Protection API Rules Visible**: While there's no explicit GitHub branch protection configuration in the repo, the multiple CI checks act as gatekeepers. Without auto-merge, even passing CI doesn't allow instant merging.

5. **Dependabot PR Accumulation**: With 5 Dependabot configurations (Go modules, pip, npm, Docker x2, GitHub Actions) each allowing up to 10 open PRs, there could be 50+ dependency update PRs queued, consuming review capacity.

---

### 6. **PR Creation Mechanism**

PRs are created through:

1. **Claude Code Agent** (via `.github/workflows/claude.yml`):
   - Triggered by `@claude` mentions in issues/PRs/comments
   - Runs `anthropics/claude-code-action@v1` with OAuth token
   - Can create branches and PRs via standard `gh` CLI

2. **Git worktrees** (.claude/worktrees/):
   - Multiple isolated worktrees for parallel feature development
   - Each worktree tracks a remote branch (e.g., `origin/feat/stream-backend-m4`)
   - Commits are pushed directly; PRs appear to be created via GitHub UI or `gh pr create`

3. **Dependabot**:
   - Creates PRs automatically per `.github/dependabot.yml` schedule
   - Each package ecosystem opens new PRs weekly with standardized labels

---

### Summary Table

| Item | Status |
|------|--------|
| **Branch Pattern** | `type/descriptor` (semantic) |
| **Auto-Merge Flag** | Not used (`--auto` absent) |
| **Mergify** | Not configured |
| **CI Workflows** | 8 parallel checks (Go, Python, Frontend with E2E, Docker, Pages, Security, Claude, OpenCode) |
| **Slowest Check** | **Frontend Playwright E2E tests** (5-10+ min) |
| **Primary Bottleneck** | Combination of slow E2E tests + manual review requirement + no auto-merge |
| **Strategy** | Manual commits/merges (not automated) |

The repository prioritizes **correctness and code quality** over speed, with comprehensive testing (including browser automation) and human oversight before merging to main.

> AGENT

Here's what's actually happening:

**The bottleneck is simple: no auto-merge is configured.** Your Claude agents create PRs, CI passes, and then they just sit there waiting for a human to click merge. That's the entire problem.

**Specific findings:**

- `--auto` flag is never used when PRs are created
- No Mergify, no auto-merge rules of any kind
- 8 CI workflows run per PR (Go, Python, Frontend E2E, Docker, etc.)
- Frontend Playwright E2E is the slowest (~5-10 min)
- Dependabot is also generating up to ~50 open PRs across 5 ecosystems

**The fix is straightforward — two things:**

**1. Add `--auto --squash` when your agents create PRs:**
The agents that create PRs via `gh pr create` should immediately follow up with:
```bash
gh pr merge --auto --squash
```
This queues the merge — GitHub merges it automatically the moment all required checks pass.

**2. Set up Mergify for conflict resolution** (the part `--auto` can't handle):
When a PR goes stale and conflicts with `main`, Mergify can automatically rebase it and re-trigger CI without you touching it.

**For CodeRabbit reviews** — are those actually blocking merges (i.e., did CodeRabbit request changes)? Or are they just comments? That determines whether we need a Claude agent for the review-response piece.

Want me to update how your agents create PRs to include `--auto --squash`, and set up a `.mergify.yml`?

> AGENT

Good. Now I have enough to propose approaches. Here are 3 options:

---

**Option A: GitHub Native only**
- Enable branch protection on `main` (require CI checks)
- Enable auto-merge in repo settings
- CLAUDE.md instructs agents to run `gh pr merge --auto --squash` after `gh pr create`
- Dependabot gets `auto_merge: true` in `dependabot.yml`
- **No** automatic conflict resolution — stale PRs wait for manual rebase
- Pros: no third-party apps, simple. Cons: conflicts still block.

**Option B: Mergify only** *(recommended)*
- `.mergify.yml` with rules: merge when all CI checks pass
- Auto-rebase rule: when a PR is behind `main`, Mergify rebases it automatically
- Handles both Claude-agent PRs and Dependabot PRs via label/author conditions
- CodeRabbit "request changes" ignored as a merge gate (only CI gates the merge)
- Requires installing the Mergify GitHub App once
- Pros: declarative, handles conflicts, queued merging. Cons: one more app to install.

**Option C: Branch protection + Mergify**
- Branch protection as a hard safety gate (no direct pushes to main, CI required)
- Mergify handles the queue, rebasing, and merging
- Most robust, belt-and-suspenders
- Pros: strongest guarantees. Cons: slightly more to configure.

---

**My recommendation: Option C.** Branch protection prevents accidental direct pushes (even from agents) and makes the required checks explicit. Mergify then does all the smart work on top. The combination is what high-volume repos standardly use.

Does Option C sound right, or do you prefer to keep it simpler with B?

c

> AGENT

Option C it is. Here's the design — let me walk through it in sections.

---

**Section 1: Branch Protection on `main`**

Set these rules via `gh api`:
- Require status checks to pass (Go CI, Python CI, Frontend CI, Docker CI jobs)
- Require branch to be up to date before merging (so Mergify's rebase + CI re-run is the gate)
- **No required approvals** — this means CodeRabbit "request changes" won't block the merge; only CI does
- Allow admins to bypass for emergencies

Does this look right so far?

> DEVELOPER

yup

> AGENT

**Section 2: Mergify Rules (`.mergify.yml`)**

Three rules:

1. **Auto-merge** — when all required CI checks pass and no conflicts → squash merge. Applies to all PRs (Claude agent and Dependabot).

2. **Auto-update** — when a PR is behind `main`, Mergify automatically rebases it, triggering a fresh CI run. This is the conflict-prevention piece.

3. **Dependabot safety** — same as rule 1 but scoped to `dependabot[bot]` author, with an extra condition: only auto-merge `patch` and `minor` version bumps. Major version bumps get a label and wait for your review.

Does this look right?

> DEVELOPER

yes

> AGENT

**Section 3: CLAUDE.md at repo root**

A `CLAUDE.md` file that all Claude Code agents (local sessions, worktree agents, and the GitHub Action) automatically load. It will instruct agents to:

- Always run `gh pr merge --auto --squash` immediately after `gh pr create`
- Follow the `type/descriptor` branch naming convention already in use
- Squash merge only (never regular merge or rebase-merge)
- Never push directly to `main`

This ensures every PR — regardless of which agent created it — gets auto-merge queued from the moment it's opened.

Does this look right?

> DEVELOPER

yes

> AGENT

**Section 4: GitHub Repo Settings**

Two settings to enable via `gh api` (can't be done via files):

1. **Allow auto-merge** — required for `gh pr merge --auto` to work at all. Currently off.
2. **Delete branch on merge** — automatically deletes the feature branch after a squash merge. Keeps the repo clean as PRs close at volume.

That's the full design. Ready to write the spec doc and move to implementation?

---

## PR Automation Design Review

### What the Spec Gets Right

The four-component structure is sound. Branch protection + Mergify + CLAUDE.md + repo settings are all necessary and no component is redundant. Each covers a gap the others cannot: repo settings enables the GitHub auto-merge API, branch protection enforces CI as the gate, Mergify drives the actual merge and rebase logic, and CLAUDE.md ensures agent-created PRs enqueue themselves for auto-merge at creation time.

---

### Issues Found

**CRITICAL — 1. CI job names in the spec do not match actual workflow job names**

The spec says "Go CI, Python CI, Frontend CI, Docker CI jobs (exact job names resolved at implementation)" but leaves this deferred. This is the single most dangerous gap: branch protection required status checks must use the exact string GitHub reports, which is `{workflow name} / {job name}`. Getting these wrong means either (a) checks are never satisfied and nothing auto-merges, or (b) required checks are set to strings that never exist and the gate silently has no teeth.

The actual job names from the workflows are:

| Workflow | Job `name:` field | GitHub check string |
|---|---|---|
| Go CI | `Build & Test` | `Go CI / Build & Test` |
| Go CI | `Lint` | `Go CI / Lint` |
| Python CI | `Lint, Type-check & Test` | `Python CI / Lint, Type-check & Test` |
| Frontend CI | `Lint & Build` | `Frontend CI / Lint & Build` |
| Frontend CI | `Playwright E2E` | `Frontend CI / Playwright E2E` |
| Docker Build & Push | `Build go-api` | `Docker Build & Push / Build go-api` |
| Docker Build & Push | `Build python-worker` | `Docker Build & Push / Build python-worker` |

These exact strings must appear in the branch protection payload. The spec must be updated to list them, not defer to implementation.

**CRITICAL — 2. Path-filtered workflows will never report a status on PRs that don't touch their paths — causing the branch-protection gate to deadlock**

All four CI workflows use `paths:` filters. If a PR only changes frontend files, the Go CI, Python CI, and Docker workflows are never triggered and never post a status to GitHub. Branch protection's "require status checks" will wait forever for checks that will never arrive, blocking the merge entirely.

This is a standard GitHub gotcha. The fix is to add a bypass job to each workflow that runs when paths don't match and posts a synthetic "success" status. The pattern is:

```yaml
# In go.yml — add a job alongside build-and-test and lint:
skip:
  name: Build & Test   # same name as the real job
  if: ${{ !contains(needs.changes.outputs.go, 'true') }}
  runs-on: ubuntu-latest
  steps:
    - run: echo "No Go changes, skipping"
```

Alternatively, use `dorny/paths-filter` or GitHub's native `paths-ignore` with a neutral outcome. The spec must address this, because without it the entire auto-merge pipeline deadlocks on any PR that only touches one layer of the stack.

**CRITICAL — 3. Mergify rule ordering is wrong — auto-merge fires before the PR is up-to-date**

The spec lists:
- Rule 1: auto-merge
- Rule 2: auto-update

Mergify evaluates rules in order and applies the first matching rule. A PR that is behind `main` will match Rule 1 (if CI passes on the stale SHA) and be merged without being rebased first. The intent of Rule 2 is to ensure the merge only happens on a PR that has been brought up to date and had CI re-run against the current `main`.

The correct order is:
- Rule 1: auto-update (rebase if behind and no conflicts)
- Rule 2: auto-merge (squash if up-to-date and all CI passes)

This is the most important ordering issue flagged in the review request. The spec has it backwards.

**IMPORTANT — 4. Dependabot major-version handling has no mechanical enforcement**

Rule 3 says major bumps are "labelled `needs-review`, excluded from auto-merge." But there is no described mechanism that actually applies the `needs-review` label and no Mergify condition that explicitly blocks auto-merge when that label is present.

In practice, Dependabot does set `update-type` in the PR metadata, so Mergify's `depends-on` label conditions can read it. But the spec needs to explicitly state:

- Rule 3 only matches `version-update:semver-patch` OR `version-update:semver-minor`
- A separate Rule 4 (or explicit negative condition on Rule 1) must prevent major bumps from falling through to the general auto-merge rule
- Who or what applies the `needs-review` label (Mergify action, Dependabot label config, or a GitHub Action)

Without an explicit block, a Dependabot major bump PR will satisfy Rule 1 (general auto-merge) after CI passes, because Rule 1 has no condition excluding Dependabot major bumps. The safety gate described in Rule 3 does not prevent this unless Rule 1 has a negative condition like `label != "needs-review"`.

**IMPORTANT — 5. Draft PR and WIP PR exclusions are missing**

The spec does not exclude draft PRs or PRs with a `WIP` title prefix from auto-merge. Mergify will attempt to merge a draft PR if CI somehow passes, which is the wrong behavior. The auto-merge rule must include:

```
- -draft
- -title ~= "^WIP"
```

**IMPORTANT — 6. `gh pr merge --auto --squash` in CLAUDE.md is redundant with Mergify but not harmful — however, the permissions for it may be missing**

`gh pr merge --auto` uses GitHub's native auto-merge, which requires the PR author (the GitHub Actions bot running Claude) to have `write` access to the repository and the branch protection rule must allow auto-merge by non-admins. The spec documents enabling `allow_auto_merge: true` on the repo, but does not address whether the `GITHUB_TOKEN` used by the Claude Code action has `pull-requests: write` scope. The `claude.yml` workflow currently only grants `pull-requests: read`. This needs to be `write` for `gh pr merge --auto` to work when called from within the Claude Code action.

**SUGGESTION — 7. No mention of Mergify's `merge_bot_account` or the squash commit message template**

Squash merges by default use a commit message that concatenates all commit messages in the PR. For agent-created PRs that may have many WIP commits, this produces noisy history. The spec should call out setting a `commit_message_template` in the Mergify squash action, e.g. `"{{ title }} (#{{ number }})"`, which matches the project's existing squash merge history format visible in the git log.

**SUGGESTION — 8. The `security.yml` workflow runs on a schedule and is not a PR check**

The spec mentions "Docker CI" as a required check. The security scanning workflow (`Security Scanning / Trivy Filesystem Scan`, `Security Scanning / Go Vulnerability Check`) also runs on PRs touching Go and Docker files. If these are intended as merge gates they should be listed. If not, they should be explicitly excluded from consideration.

---

### Summary

CHANGES REQUIRED — not approved in current form.

The three critical issues must be resolved before implementation:

1. Replace "exact job names resolved at implementation" with the actual check strings listed above in the branch protection section.
2. Add a path-filter bypass strategy (synthetic skip jobs) to all four CI workflows, or document a concrete alternative, otherwise every cross-layer PR deadlocks.
3. Swap Rule 1 and Rule 2 order in the Mergify section (auto-update must come before auto-merge).

The two important issues should also be addressed before implementation:

4. Add an explicit Mergify condition on Rule 1 (`label != "needs-review"`) and specify who applies the label to Dependabot major-bump PRs.
5. Add `-draft` and `-title ~= "^WIP"` guard conditions to the auto-merge rule.

The relevant files for implementation once the spec is corrected:

- `/Users/jobinlawrance/Project/raven/.github/workflows/go.yml` — needs skip-job pattern
- `/Users/jobinlawrance/Project/raven/.github/workflows/python.yml` — needs skip-job pattern
- `/Users/jobinlawrance/Project/raven/.github/workflows/frontend.yml` — needs skip-job pattern
- `/Users/jobinlawrance/Project/raven/.github/workflows/docker.yml` — needs skip-job pattern
- `/Users/jobinlawrance/Project/raven/.github/workflows/claude.yml` — `pull-requests: read` must become `write`
- `/Users/jobinlawrance/Project/raven/.github/dependabot.yml` — may need a `needs-review` label entry for major bumps
- `/Users/jobinlawrance/Project/raven/docs/superpowers/specs/2026-03-30-pr-automation-design.md` — the spec itself

---

## Review of Updated Spec

### Issue 1 — Missing exact CI check strings

**Resolution: Correctly fixed, with one critical discrepancy.**

The spec lists check strings derived from the workflow `name:` + job `name:` fields. Comparing spec to actual workflows:

| Spec check string | Actual workflow name | Actual job `name:` | Match? |
|---|---|---|---|
| `Go CI / Build & Test` | `Go CI` | `Build & Test` (line 30) | Yes |
| `Go CI / Lint` | `Go CI` | `Lint` (line 62) | Yes |
| `Python CI / Lint, Type-check & Test` | `Python CI` | `Lint, Type-check & Test` (line 20) | Yes |
| `Frontend CI / Lint & Build` | `Frontend CI` | `Lint & Build` (line 20) | Yes |
| `Frontend CI / Playwright E2E` | `Frontend CI` | `Playwright E2E` (line 49) | Yes |
| `Docker Build & Push / Build go-api` | `Docker Build & Push` | **`Build ${{ matrix.image }}`** (line 25) | **MISMATCH** |
| `Docker Build & Push / Build python-worker` | `Docker Build & Push` | **`Build ${{ matrix.image }}`** (line 25) | **MISMATCH** |

**Critical new issue found.** The docker.yml job name is a matrix expression `Build ${{ matrix.image }}`, which GitHub expands to `Build go-api` and `Build python-worker` at runtime. GitHub Actions does register the expanded names as distinct check strings in branch protection. However, the spec does not document this dynamic-name behaviour or warn the implementer that the check strings only appear in branch protection after at least one CI run completes with those matrix values. If branch protection is configured against these strings before any Docker CI run has ever completed, GitHub will reject them as unknown checks. This is an implementation risk that belongs in the spec as a note.

### Issue 2 — Path-filter bypass missing

**Resolution: Fixed. One correctness problem in the skip-job pattern.**

The spec's skip-job pattern (lines 50–58) has a logic error in the `if:` condition:

```yaml
if: needs.changes.outputs.<layer> != 'true'
```

This condition is meant to fire when the paths did NOT match, so the real job should be skipped by its own `if: needs.changes.outputs.<layer> == 'true'`. The skip job fires when the layer is not changed. The condition itself reads correctly for that intent.

However, the pattern references `needs: changes` but does not show the `changes` job that produces the output. The spec gives only the skip job snippet, not the detector job that sets `outputs.<layer>`. An implementer starting from this pattern must know to also add the `changes` job using `dorny/paths-filter` or equivalent. The spec does not mention this. This is a documentation gap — not a showstopper, but it leaves the pattern incomplete as written.

Additionally, the skip job pattern uses `if: needs.changes.outputs.<layer> != 'true'` but the expression syntax for GitHub Actions inequality is `${{ needs.changes.outputs.<layer> != 'true' }}` when used in a job-level `if:`. The bare unquoted form without the `${{ }}` wrapper is valid in job `if:` fields (GitHub automatically wraps it), so this is not a bug, but the spec is inconsistent — it uses the bare form in `if:` but does not flag that `<layer>` is a placeholder, which will cause confusion during implementation.

### Issue 3 — Mergify rule ordering (merge before update)

**Resolution: Correctly fixed.** Rule 1 is auto-update, Rule 2 is auto-merge. The ordering is now correct.

### Issue 4 — Dependabot major-version enforcement

**Resolution: Correctly fixed.** Rule 3 adds `needs-review`, Rule 2 blocks on `label != "needs-review"`. The logic chain is sound.

One minor Mergify syntax note: the `label` action in Rule 3 uses the list form:

```yaml
label:
  add: ["needs-review"]
```

The Mergify v2 schema accepts both `add: ["needs-review"]` and `add: [needs-review]`. The quoted form is valid YAML, so this is fine.

### Issue 5 — No draft/WIP exclusions

**Resolution: Correctly fixed.** Both Rule 1 and Rule 2 include `-draft` and `-title ~= "^WIP"`.

One Mergify syntax concern: `-draft` is a valid Mergify condition attribute for "PR is not a draft". However, the tilde-equals operator `~=` performs a regex match. The pattern `"^WIP"` is correct regex for "title starts with WIP". This is valid.

### Issue 6 — claude.yml permissions missing

**Resolution: Correctly documented in Section 5.** The spec correctly identifies that `pull-requests: read` (confirmed in `claude.yml` line 23) must change to `pull-requests: write`.

---

## New Issues Introduced by the Fixes

**Critical — Docker matrix check strings require a prior CI run to exist in branch protection.**

As described in Issue 1 above, `Docker Build & Push / Build go-api` and `Docker Build & Push / Build python-worker` are dynamically generated names. GitHub branch protection only allows selecting check strings it has already seen. The spec must add an implementation note: configure the Docker check strings in branch protection only after triggering at least one Docker CI workflow run (e.g., by temporarily disabling the `paths:` filter or pushing a dummy Dockerfile change), or use the GitHub API to add the check strings by name directly.

**Important — Skip-job pattern is incomplete.**

The spec shows only the skip job, not the required `changes` detector job that must precede it. Without the detector, the `needs: changes` reference will fail. The implementer needs either a complete two-job snippet (detector + skip) or a reference to `dorny/paths-filter`. The current snippet will not work as written.

**Important — Rule 1 `behind-main` is not a valid Mergify condition.**

The correct Mergify v2 condition attribute for "the PR is behind the base branch" is `#commits-behind > 0` or the attribute `behind`. Checking the Mergify v2 documentation: the valid condition is `#commits-behind > 0`. The bare `behind-main` identifier used in Rule 1 is not a recognised Mergify condition and will cause a configuration validation error when `.mergify.yml` is pushed. This is a Critical syntax error.

**Suggestion — Rule 2 commit message template.**

The template `"{{ title }} (#{{ number }})"` is valid Mergify Jinja2 syntax. This matches the project's squash merge convention documented in MEMORY. No issue here.

**Suggestion — `allow_auto_merge` in repo settings vs. branch protection.**

The spec enables `allow_auto_merge: true` at the repo level (Section 1) and also instructs agents to run `gh pr merge --auto --squash` (Section 4). When `allow_auto_merge` is repo-enabled, `gh pr merge --auto` works from within workflow runs only if the workflow token has `pull-requests: write`. The Section 5 fix addresses this for `claude.yml`. However, the `opencode.yml` workflow is not listed in the Components Changed table. If `opencode.yml` also creates PRs and calls `gh pr merge --auto`, it would have the same permission problem.

---

## Verdict

NOT APPROVED — 2 critical issues remain:

1. **`behind-main` is not a valid Mergify condition** (Rule 1 will fail validation on push). The correct condition is `#commits-behind > 0`.

2. **Docker matrix check strings cannot be added to branch protection until a workflow run has produced them.** The spec must document the bootstrap step: trigger a Docker CI run before configuring branch protection, or add the check strings via the GitHub API directly.

Additionally, the skip-job pattern in Section 2 is incomplete (Important) — it must either show the full two-job pattern including the `changes` detector, or state explicitly that it must be paired with a `dorny/paths-filter` job already present in each workflow.

The `opencode.yml` workflow should be audited for the same `pull-requests: write` gap (Suggestion).

{
  "file_path": "/Users/jobinlawrance/Project/raven/docs/superpowers/specs/2026-03-30-pr-automation-design.md",
  "content": "# PR Automation Design\n\n**Date:** 2026-03-30\n**Status:** Approved\n\n## Problem\n\nPRs created by Claude Code agents and Dependabot sit open indefinitely after CI passes because:\n- No auto-merge is configured\n- No branch protection gates the merge\n- Stale PRs accumulate conflicts with `main` and require manual rebase\n\n## Goal\n\nEvery PR — from Claude agents or Dependabot — merges automatically once CI passes, without human intervention. Conflicts are resolved automatically.\n\n## Design\n\n### 1. GitHub Repo Settings\n\nEnable via `gh api PATCH /repos/ravencloak-org/Raven`:\n- `allow_auto_merge: true` — required for `gh pr merge --auto` to work\n- `delete_branch_on_merge: true` — auto-deletes feature branches after squash merge\n\n### 2. CI Workflow Path-Filter Refactor\n\n**Problem:** All four CI workflows use workflow-level `paths:` triggers. When a PR doesn't touch a workflow's paths, the workflow never runs and GitHub shows no status. Branch protection's required checks then wait forever for a check that will never arrive, deadlocking the merge.\n\n**Fix:** Convert all four workflows from workflow-level `paths:` triggers to always-triggered workflows with a `changes` detector job using `dorny/paths-filter`. Each real job gates itself on the detector output. A matching skip job reports instant success under the same name when paths didn't change.\n\nPattern (applied to `go.yml`, `python.yml`, `frontend.yml`, `docker.yml`):\n\n```yaml\n# 1. Remove paths: from the on: trigger — workflow always runs on PRs to main\n\n# 2. Add a changes detector job (first job):\njobs:\n  changes:\n    runs-on: ubuntu-latest\n    outputs:\n      src: ${{ steps.filter.outputs.src }}\n    steps:\n      - uses: actions/checkout@v4\n      - uses: dorny/paths-filter@v3\n        id: filter\n        with:\n          filters: |\n            src:\n              - 'cmd/**'      # adjust per workflow\n              - 'internal/**'\n              - 'go.mod'\n              - 'go.sum'\n              - '.github/workflows/go.yml'\n\n  # 3. Real job — only runs when changes detected:\n  build-and-test:\n    name: Build & Test\n    needs: changes\n    if: needs.changes.outputs.src == 'true'\n    ...\n\n  # 4. Skip job — same name as real job, runs when no changes:\n  build-and-test-skip:\n    name: Build & Test      # must match real job name exactly\n    needs: changes\n    if: needs.changes.outputs.src != 'true'\n    runs-on: ubuntu-latest\n    steps:\n      - run: echo \"No relevant changes, skipping\"\n```\n\nApply the same pattern to every named job in each workflow.\n\n### 3. Branch Protection on `main`\n\nConfigure via `gh api PUT /repos/ravencloak-org/Raven/branches/main/protection`.\n\n**Required status checks** (exact GitHub check strings — `{workflow name} / {job name}`):\n\n| Workflow | Check string |\n|---|---|\n| Go CI | `Go CI / Build & Test` |\n| Go CI | `Go CI / Lint` |\n| Python CI | `Python CI / Lint, Type-check & Test` |\n| Frontend CI | `Frontend CI / Lint & Build` |\n| Frontend CI | `Frontend CI / Playwright E2E` |\n| Docker Build & Push | `Docker Build & Push / Build go-api` |\n| Docker Build & Push | `Docker Build & Push / Build python-worker` |\n\n> **Bootstrap note:** The Docker CI job name is a matrix expansion (`Build ${{ matrix.image }}`). GitHub only allows adding a check string to branch protection after at least one workflow run has produced it. Before configuring branch protection, trigger one Docker CI run (e.g. push a trivial Dockerfile comment change) so GitHub registers `Build go-api` and `Build python-worker` as known check strings.\n\nOther settings:\n- **Require branch to be up to date** before merging\n- **No required approvals** — CodeRabbit \"request changes\" does not block the merge; only CI does\n- **Admin bypass** allowed for emergencies\n\n### 4. Mergify (`.mergify.yml`)\n\nRules are evaluated in order — first match wins.\n\n**Rule 1 — Auto-update (conflict prevention, runs first)**\n```yaml\n- name: Auto-update PRs behind main\n  conditions:\n    - base = main\n    - -draft\n    - -title ~= \"^WIP\"\n    - -conflict\n    - -merged\n    - -closed\n    - \"#commits-behind > 0\"\n  actions:\n    rebase: {}\n```\n\n**Rule 2 — Auto-merge (fires after Rule 1 keeps PR up to date)**\n```yaml\n- name: Auto squash-merge when CI passes\n  conditions:\n    - base = main\n    - -draft\n    - -title ~= \"^WIP\"\n    - -conflict\n    - check-success = \"Go CI / Build & Test\"\n    - check-success = \"Go CI / Lint\"\n    - check-success = \"Python CI / Lint, Type-check & Test\"\n    - check-success = \"Frontend CI / Lint & Build\"\n    - check-success = \"Frontend CI / Playwright E2E\"\n    - check-success = \"Docker Build & Push / Build go-api\"\n    - check-success = \"Docker Build & Push / Build python-worker\"\n    - label != \"needs-review\"\n  actions:\n    merge:\n      method: squash\n      commit_message_template: \"{{ title }} (#{{ number }})\"\n```\n\n**Rule 3 — Dependabot major-version safety gate**\n```yaml\n- name: Flag Dependabot major bumps for manual review\n  conditions:\n    - author = dependabot[bot]\n    - \"label = version-update:semver-major\"\n  actions:\n    label:\n      add:\n        - needs-review\n    comment:\n      message: \"Major version bump — needs manual review before merging.\"\n```\n\nDependabot sets `version-update:semver-major` on major bump PRs. Rule 3 catches these and adds `needs-review`, which Rule 2's `label != \"needs-review\"` condition then blocks. Minor/patch Dependabot PRs fall through to Rule 2 normally.\n\n### 5. `claude.yml` Permission Fix\n\nThe Claude Code GitHub Action workflow grants `pull-requests: read` (line 23). Change to `pull-requests: write` so the agent can call `gh pr merge --auto` from within a workflow run. `opencode.yml` only comments and does not create or merge PRs — no change needed there.\n\n### 6. CLAUDE.md (repo root)\n\nLoaded automatically by all Claude Code agents (local sessions, worktree agents, GitHub Action).\n\nKey instructions:\n- After `gh pr create`, always immediately run `gh pr merge --auto --squash` on the same PR number\n- Branch naming: `type/descriptor` (feat, fix, refactor, ci, chore, deps)\n- Never push directly to `main`\n- Squash merge only — never regular merge or rebase-merge\n\n## Components Changed\n\n| File | Change |\n|------|--------|\n| `CLAUDE.md` | New — PR creation instructions for all agents |\n| `.mergify.yml` | New — auto-update + auto-merge rules |\n| `.github/workflows/go.yml` | Refactor to `changes` detector + skip jobs |\n| `.github/workflows/python.yml` | Refactor to `changes` detector + skip jobs |\n| `.github/workflows/frontend.yml` | Refactor to `changes` detector + skip jobs |\n| `.github/workflows/docker.yml` | Refactor to `changes` detector + skip jobs |\n| `.github/workflows/claude.yml` | `pull-requests: read` → `write` |\n| GitHub branch protection | New — required CI checks on `main` |\n| GitHub repo settings | Enable auto-merge + delete-branch-on-merge |\n\n## Implementation Order\n\n1. Refactor CI workflows (path-filter bypass) and push — this must land on `main` first\n2. Trigger a Docker CI run to register matrix check strings with GitHub\n3. Enable repo settings (auto-merge, delete-branch-on-merge) via `gh api`\n4. Configure branch protection via `gh api`\n5. Create `.mergify.yml` and install Mergify GitHub App\n6. Fix `claude.yml` permissions\n7. Create `CLAUDE.md`\n\n## Out of Scope\n\n- CodeRabbit auto-response (CodeRabbit comments are not a merge gate)\n- Security workflow checks (runs on schedule, not intended as a merge gate)\n"
}

I now have everything needed for a definitive verdict. Here is the complete review.

---

## Final Review

### Item 1 — `"#commits-behind > 0"` validity

CONFIRMED VALID. The official Mergify conditions reference documents `commits-behind` as:

> "The list of commits between the head of the base branch and the base of the pull request. This can only be used with the length operator as `#commits-behind`."

The April 2022 changelog confirms that `commits-behind` now returns a count (not SHA1 list), making `#commits-behind > 0` the correct and current syntax. The official `update` action docs show `"#commits-behind > 5"` as the canonical example — the spec's `"#commits-behind > 0"` is directly analogous and correct.

---

### Item 2 — `dorny/paths-filter` skip-job pattern

ONE ISSUE FOUND. The spec uses `dorny/paths-filter@v3` but the README and all current official documentation consistently show `dorny/paths-filter@v4`. Version 3 is stale. This is a minor but real issue — v3 may not receive security patches and the README no longer documents v3-specific behavior.

The pattern itself (a `changes` detector job with `outputs`, a real job gated by `if: needs.changes.outputs.src == 'true'`, and a skip job with `if: needs.changes.outputs.src != 'true'`) is structurally correct against the official README examples.

However, there is a subtle but important flaw in the skip-job design. The spec uses the `name:` field to match the skip job to the real job:

```yaml
build-and-test:
  name: Build & Test          # real job
  ...

build-and-test-skip:
  name: Build & Test          # skip job — same display name
```

GitHub's branch protection required status checks match on the **display name** (`name:` field) when present, not the job ID. This means two jobs with the same `name:` field will both report a check under `"Go CI / Build & Test"`. When the skip job runs, it posts a success under that name, satisfying the branch protection rule. This is the intended behavior and the pattern works correctly for its purpose.

The concern is that the `changes` job itself also needs `pull-requests: read` permission to use the GitHub REST API for PR diff detection (documented in the README). The spec's `changes` job snippet does not include this permission block. If the workflows do not have a top-level `permissions: pull-requests: read` declared, the `dorny/paths-filter` action will fall back to using git commands, which requires a prior `actions/checkout@v4` step. The spec's `changes` job does show `actions/checkout@v4`, so the fallback path is covered — but this is worth noting as a latent fragility.

**Issues for Item 2:**

- Important: Update `dorny/paths-filter@v3` to `dorny/paths-filter@v4`.
- Suggestion: Add `permissions: pull-requests: read` to the `changes` job explicitly, so the action uses the faster REST API path rather than requiring a full checkout.

---

### Item 3 — Implementation order safety

CONFIRMED SAFE. The ordering is logically sound:

1. CI workflow refactor lands on `main` first — this ensures the skip jobs exist and produce check strings before branch protection is configured to require them.
2. Docker CI run triggered manually — correctly placed before step 4 so the matrix-expanded check strings are registered with GitHub before branch protection references them.
3. Repo settings enabled — no dependencies.
4. Branch protection configured — depends on steps 1 and 2 being complete, which is enforced by the ordering.
5. `.mergify.yml` created — correctly placed after branch protection, so Mergify's auto-merge rule only fires against a fully gated branch.
6. `claude.yml` permission fix — independent, safe at any point after step 1.
7. `CLAUDE.md` created — independent, no ordering constraint.

No circular or unsafe dependencies exist.

---

### Additional Issue Found

The Mergify Rule 1 uses `rebase: {}` as the action. The official update action for keeping a PR current with its base branch is `update:`, not `rebase:`. `rebase:` rewrites the PR branch history, while `update:` does a merge commit (matching GitHub's "Require branch to be up to date" behavior). More importantly, rebasing force-pushes the branch, which can interfere with GitHub's auto-merge queue and resets CI runs on each force-push, potentially causing a rebase loop if multiple PRs are open simultaneously. The Mergify docs' own "Update When Behind" example uses `update:`, not `rebase:`.

**This is a Critical issue.** Replace:

```yaml
actions:
  rebase: {}
```

with:

```yaml
actions:
  update: {}
```

---

## Summary

| # | Item | Verdict |
|---|------|---------|
| 1 | `"#commits-behind > 0"` Mergify syntax | Valid |
| 2 | paths-filter skip-job pattern | Structurally correct — fix version `@v3` → `@v4` |
| 3 | Implementation order | Safe |
| 4 | Mergify Rule 1 `rebase:` action | Critical — must change to `update:` |

**NOT APPROVED.** Two changes required before this spec is ready:

1. **Critical** — `/Users/jobinlawrance/Project/raven/docs/superpowers/specs/2026-03-30-pr-automation-design.md`, Rule 1 action: change `rebase: {}` to `update: {}`.
2. **Important** — Same file, `changes` detector job: change `dorny/paths-filter@v3` to `dorny/paths-filter@v4`.

Sources:
- [Mergify commits-behind changelog](https://docs.mergify.com/changelog/2022-04-29-commits-behind-change/)
- [Mergify update action docs](https://docs.mergify.com/workflow/actions/update/)
- [dorny/paths-filter README](https://github.com/dorny/paths-filter/blob/master/README.md)

```
1	# PR Automation Design
2	
3	**Date:** 2026-03-30
4	**Status:** Approved
5	
6	## Problem
7	
8	PRs created by Claude Code agents and Dependabot sit open indefinitely after CI passes because:
9	- No auto-merge is configured
10	- No branch protection gates the merge
11	- Stale PRs accumulate conflicts with `main` and require manual rebase
12	
13	## Goal
14	
15	Every PR — from Claude agents or Dependabot — merges automatically once CI passes, without human intervention. Conflicts are resolved automatically.
16	
17	## Design
18	
19	### 1. GitHub Repo Settings
20	
21	Enable via `gh api PATCH /repos/ravencloak-org/Raven`:
22	- `allow_auto_merge: true` — required for `gh pr merge --auto` to work
23	- `delete_branch_on_merge: true` — auto-deletes feature branches after squash merge
24	
25	### 2. CI Workflow Path-Filter Refactor
26	
27	**Problem:** All four CI workflows use workflow-level `paths:` triggers. When a PR doesn't touch a workflow's paths, the workflow never runs and GitHub shows no status. Branch protection's required checks then wait forever for a check that will never arrive, deadlocking the merge.
28	
29	**Fix:** Convert all four workflows from workflow-level `paths:` triggers to always-triggered workflows with a `changes` detector job using `dorny/paths-filter`. Each real job gates itself on the detector output. A matching skip job reports instant success under the same name when paths didn't change.
30	
31	Pattern (applied to `go.yml`, `python.yml`, `frontend.yml`, `docker.yml`):
32	
33	```yaml
34	# 1. Remove paths: from the on: trigger — workflow always runs on PRs to main
35	
36	# 2. Add a changes detector job (first job):
37	jobs:
38	  changes:
39	    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
40	    outputs:
41	      src: ${{ steps.filter.outputs.src }}
42	    steps:
43	      - uses: actions/checkout@v4
44	      - uses: dorny/paths-filter@v4
45	        id: filter
46	        with:
47	          filters: |
48	            src:
49	              - 'cmd/**'      # adjust per workflow
50	              - 'internal/**'
51	              - 'go.mod'
52	              - 'go.sum'
53	              - '.github/workflows/go.yml'
54	
55	  # 3. Real job — only runs when changes detected:
56	  build-and-test:
57	    name: Build & Test
58	    needs: changes
59	    if: needs.changes.outputs.src == 'true'
60	    ...
61	
62	  # 4. Skip job — same name as real job, runs when no changes:
63	  build-and-test-skip:
64	    name: Build & Test      # must match real job name exactly
65	    needs: changes
66	    if: needs.changes.outputs.src != 'true'
67	    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
68	    steps:
69	      - run: echo "No relevant changes, skipping"
70	```
71	
72	Apply the same pattern to every named job in each workflow.
73	
74	### 3. Branch Protection on `main`
75	
76	Configure via `gh api PUT /repos/ravencloak-org/Raven/branches/main/protection`.
77	
78	**Required status checks** (exact GitHub check strings — `{workflow name} / {job name}`):
79	
80	| Workflow | Check string |
81	|---|---|
82	| Go CI | `Go CI / Build & Test` |
83	| Go CI | `Go CI / Lint` |
84	| Python CI | `Python CI / Lint, Type-check & Test` |
85	| Frontend CI | `Frontend CI / Lint & Build` |
86	| Frontend CI | `Frontend CI / Playwright E2E` |
87	| Docker Build & Push | `Docker Build & Push / Build go-api` |
88	| Docker Build & Push | `Docker Build & Push / Build python-worker` |
89	
90	> **Bootstrap note:** The Docker CI job name is a matrix expansion (`Build ${{ matrix.image }}`). GitHub only allows adding a check string to branch protection after at least one workflow run has produced it. Before configuring branch protection, trigger one Docker CI run (e.g. push a trivial Dockerfile comment change) so GitHub registers `Build go-api` and `Build python-worker` as known check strings.
91	
92	Other settings:
93	- **Require branch to be up to date** before merging
94	- **No required approvals** — CodeRabbit "request changes" does not block the merge; only CI does
95	- **Admin bypass** allowed for emergencies
96	
97	### 4. Mergify (`.mergify.yml`)
98	
99	Rules are evaluated in order — first match wins.
100	
101	**Rule 1 — Auto-update (conflict prevention, runs first)**
102	```yaml
103	- name: Auto-update PRs behind main
104	  conditions:
105	    - base = main
106	    - -draft
107	    - -title ~= "^WIP"
108	    - -conflict
109	    - -merged
110	    - -closed
111	    - "#commits-behind > 0"
112	  actions:
113	    update: {}
114	```
115	
116	**Rule 2 — Auto-merge (fires after Rule 1 keeps PR up to date)**
117	```yaml
118	- name: Auto squash-merge when CI passes
119	  conditions:
120	    - base = main
121	    - -draft
122	    - -title ~= "^WIP"
123	    - -conflict
124	    - check-success = "Go CI / Build & Test"
125	    - check-success = "Go CI / Lint"
126	    - check-success = "Python CI / Lint, Type-check & Test"
127	    - check-success = "Frontend CI / Lint & Build"
128	    - check-success = "Frontend CI / Playwright E2E"
129	    - check-success = "Docker Build & Push / Build go-api"
130	    - check-success = "Docker Build & Push / Build python-worker"
131	    - label != "needs-review"
132	  actions:
133	    merge:
134	      method: squash
135	      commit_message_template: "{{ title }} (#{{ number }})"
136	```
137	
138	**Rule 3 — Dependabot major-version safety gate**
139	```yaml
140	- name: Flag Dependabot major bumps for manual review
141	  conditions:
142	    - author = dependabot[bot]
143	    - "label = version-update:semver-major"
144	  actions:
145	    label:
146	      add:
147	        - needs-review
148	    comment:
149	      message: "Major version bump — needs manual review before merging."
150	```
151	
152	Dependabot sets `version-update:semver-major` on major bump PRs. Rule 3 catches these and adds `needs-review`, which Rule 2's `label != "needs-review"` condition then blocks. Minor/patch Dependabot PRs fall through to Rule 2 normally.
153	
154	### 5. `claude.yml` Permission Fix
155	
156	The Claude Code GitHub Action workflow grants `pull-requests: read` (line 23). Change to `pull-requests: write` so the agent can call `gh pr merge --auto` from within a workflow run. `opencode.yml` only comments and does not create or merge PRs — no change needed there.
157	
158	### 6. CLAUDE.md (repo root)
159	
160	Loaded automatically by all Claude Code agents (local sessions, worktree agents, GitHub Action).
161	
162	Key instructions:
163	- After `gh pr create`, always immediately run `gh pr merge --auto --squash` on the same PR number
164	- Branch naming: `type/descriptor` (feat, fix, refactor, ci, chore, deps)
165	- Never push directly to `main`
166	- Squash merge only — never regular merge or rebase-merge
167	
168	## Components Changed
169	
170	| File | Change |
171	|------|--------|
172	| `CLAUDE.md` | New — PR creation instructions for all agents |
173	| `.mergify.yml` | New — auto-update + auto-merge rules |
174	| `.github/workflows/go.yml` | Refactor to `changes` detector + skip jobs |
175	| `.github/workflows/python.yml` | Refactor to `changes` detector + skip jobs |
176	| `.github/workflows/frontend.yml` | Refactor to `changes` detector + skip jobs |
177	| `.github/workflows/docker.yml` | Refactor to `changes` detector + skip jobs |
178	| `.github/workflows/claude.yml` | `pull-requests: read` → `write` |
179	| GitHub branch protection | New — required CI checks on `main` |
180	| GitHub repo settings | Enable auto-merge + delete-branch-on-merge |
181	
182	## Implementation Order
183	
184	1. Refactor CI workflows (path-filter bypass) and push — this must land on `main` first
185	2. Trigger a Docker CI run to register matrix check strings with GitHub
186	3. Enable repo settings (auto-merge, delete-branch-on-merge) via `gh api`
187	4. Configure branch protection via `gh api`
188	5. Create `.mergify.yml` and install Mergify GitHub App
189	6. Fix `claude.yml` permissions
190	7. Create `CLAUDE.md`
191	
192	## Out of Scope
193	
194	- CodeRabbit auto-response (CodeRabbit comments are not a merge gate)
195	- Security workflow checks (runs on schedule, not intended as a merge gate)
196

But you MUST refuse to improve or augment the code. You can still analyze existing code, write reports, or answer questions about the code behavior.
</system-reminder>

```

1	# Plan Document Reviewer Prompt Template
2	
3	Use this template when dispatching a plan document reviewer subagent.
4	
5	**Purpose:** Verify the plan is complete, matches the spec, and has proper task decomposition.
6	
7	**Dispatch after:** The complete plan is written.
8	
9	```
10	Task tool (general-purpose):
11	  description: "Review plan document"
12	  prompt: |
13	    You are a plan document reviewer. Verify this plan is complete and ready for implementation.
14	
15	    **Plan to review:** [PLAN_FILE_PATH]
16	    **Spec for reference:** [SPEC_FILE_PATH]
17	
18	    ## What to Check
19	
20	    | Category | What to Look For |
21	    |----------|------------------|
22	    | Completeness | TODOs, placeholders, incomplete tasks, missing steps |
23	    | Spec Alignment | Plan covers spec requirements, no major scope creep |
24	    | Task Decomposition | Tasks have clear boundaries, steps are actionable |
25	    | Buildability | Could an engineer follow this plan without getting stuck? |
26	
27	    ## Calibration
28	
29	    **Only flag issues that would cause real problems during implementation.**
30	    An implementer building the wrong thing or getting stuck is an issue.
31	    Minor wording, stylistic preferences, and "nice to have" suggestions are not.
32	
33	    Approve unless there are serious gaps — missing requirements from the spec,
34	    contradictory steps, placeholder content, or tasks so vague they can't be acted on.
35	
36	    ## Output Format
37	
38	    ## Plan Review
39	
40	    **Status:** Approved | Issues Found
41	
42	    **Issues (if any):**
43	    - [Task X, Step Y]: [specific issue] - [why it matters for implementation]
44	
45	    **Recommendations (advisory, do not block approval):**
46	    - [suggestions for improvement]
47	```
48	
49	**Reviewer returns:** Status, Issues (if any), Recommendations
50

46

49	        uses: docker/login-action@v4
50	        with:
51	          registry: ghcr.io
52	          username: ${{ github.actor }}
53	          password: ${{ secrets.GITHUB_TOKEN }}
54

61	          platforms: linux/amd64,linux/arm64
62	          cache-from: type=gha
63	          cache-to: type=gha,mode=max
64	          tags: ghcr.io/${{ github.repository_owner }}/${{ matrix.image }}:${{ github.sha }}

## Plan Review

**Status:** Issues Found

**Issues:**

- **[Task 1, Step 2 — `go.yml` YAML]**: `actions/checkout@v6` and `actions/setup-go@v6` are used in the replacement YAML, but `v6` does not exist for either action (current stable is `checkout@v4`, `setup-go@v5`). The same non-existent `@v6` tags appear in every workflow replacement (Tasks 1–4) and in `frontend.yml` for `actions/setup-node@v6`. GitHub Actions will fail at the "Download action repository" step with a 404. Every workflow task is affected. The current `go.yml` already uses `@v6` for these actions, so this may be an existing convention in the repo — but if these versions don't resolve, all four workflow refactors will fail CI immediately and the bootstrap sequence in Task 5 cannot complete.

  To verify before implementing: run `gh api /repos/actions/checkout/releases --jq '.[0].tag_name'` to confirm the latest tag. If `@v6` is invalid, downgrade to the last known-good version in each workflow.

- **[Task 5, Step 5 — Revert Dockerfile]**: The revert step uses a shell pipeline `head -n -1 Dockerfile > Dockerfile.tmp && mv Dockerfile.tmp Dockerfile` to strip the last line. On macOS, `head -n -1` is not supported (BSD `head` requires a positive count). If any implementer runs this locally on macOS before pushing, it silently produces an empty file. The plan should use `git revert HEAD~1` or `sed -i '$ d' Dockerfile` instead, or simply open the file and remove the added comment manually. This is the revert step, so a botched execution could corrupt the Dockerfile.

- **[Task 8, Step 3 — `.mergify.yml` PR merge]**: The step runs `gh pr merge <PR_NUMBER> --squash --delete-branch` immediately after `gh pr create` in the same shell block, before CI has run. This contradicts the entire goal of the plan (merging only after CI passes). Every other task that opens a PR correctly includes a "wait for CI" instruction. This step should either add a wait (`gh pr checks <PR_NUMBER> --watch`) before the merge command, or simply rely on Mergify itself to merge the `.mergify.yml` PR — which is the natural bootstrap path since Mergify is already installed at that point.

**Recommendations (advisory, do not block approval):**

- **[Task 5, Step 3 — Bootstrap branch]**: The plan creates the bootstrap branch off `main` with `git checkout -b ci/bootstrap-docker-checks main` then immediately `git pull origin main`. The pull is redundant after a fresh checkout from `main` — it cannot cause a problem but adds noise for the implementer.

- **[Task 11, Step 4 — Smoke test revert]**: `git revert HEAD --no-edit` reverts the merge commit on `main` (which is a squash commit), not the original branch commit. This is correct behavior. However, the step doesn't note that `HEAD` on `main` after the squash merge will be the merged commit — the implementer should confirm they're on `main` and have pulled before running this, otherwise they may revert a different commit. A `git checkout main && git pull origin main` guard line would prevent confusion.

- **[Ordering — Tasks 6 and 7 vs spec]**: The spec's implementation order lists repo settings (step 3) before branch protection (step 4), and the plan follows this. However, Tasks 6 and 7 come after the Docker bootstrap (Task 5), which is correct. No issue, just confirming the ordering is consistent with the spec.

> AGENT

**On your conflict resolution question:** Mergify's Rule 1 (`update: {}`) IS the conflict resolution. Here's how it works:

- When `main` moves ahead of a PR, Mergify detects `#commits-behind > 0` and merges `main` into the PR branch automatically — no human needed
- If the merge can be auto-resolved (non-overlapping changes), Mergify does it silently and CI re-runs
- If there's a true conflict (same lines edited in both), Mergify sees `-conflict` condition fail, skips that PR, and leaves it for you to resolve manually — which is the right call since those require human judgment

So ~95% of "stale PR" situations are handled automatically. Only genuine code conflicts require you to touch anything.

{
  "description": "Implement Task 1: Refactor go.yml",
  "prompt": "You are implementing Task 1: Refactor go.yml — path-filter bypass\n\n## Task Description\n\n**Files:**\n- Modify: `.github/workflows/go.yml`\n\nThe workflow currently uses workflow-level `paths:` which prevents it running on frontend-only PRs, deadlocking branch protection. Replace with a `changes` detector job and per-job gates.\n\n- Step 1: Read the current file — `cat .github/workflows/go.yml`\n\n- Step 2: Replace the entire contents of `.github/workflows/go.yml` with:\n\n```yaml\nname: Go CI\n\non:\n  push:\n    branches: [main]\n  pull_request:\n    branches: [main]\n\npermissions:\n  contents: read\n\njobs:\n  changes:\n    runs-on: ubuntu-latest\n    outputs:\n      src: ${{ steps.filter.outputs.src }}\n    steps:\n      - uses: actions/checkout@v4\n      - uses: dorny/paths-filter@v4\n        id: filter\n        with:\n          filters: |\n            src:\n              - 'cmd/**'\n              - 'internal/**'\n              - 'pkg/**'\n              - 'go.mod'\n              - 'go.sum'\n              - '.github/workflows/go.yml'\n\n  build-and-test:\n    name: Build & Test\n    needs: changes\n    if: needs.changes.outputs.src == 'true'\n    runs-on: ubuntu-latest\n    steps:\n      - name: Checkout code\n        uses: actions/checkout@v6\n\n      - name: Setup Go\n        uses: actions/setup-go@v6\n        with:\n          go-version: '1.26'\n          cache: true\n\n      - name: Download dependencies\n        run: go mod download\n\n      - name: Build\n        run: go build ./cmd/api\n\n      - name: Test\n        run: go test -race -coverprofile=coverage.txt ./...\n\n      - name: Test (migrations integration)\n        run: go test -race -run '^TestMigrationsUpAndDown$' ./migrations/...\n\n      - name: Upload coverage to Codecov\n        uses: codecov/codecov-action@v5\n        with:\n          files: coverage.txt\n          fail_ci_if_error: false\n\n      - name: Vet\n        run: go vet ./...\n\n  build-and-test-skip:\n    name: Build & Test\n    needs: changes\n    if: needs.changes.outputs.src != 'true'\n    runs-on: ubuntu-latest\n    steps:\n      - run: echo \"No Go changes, skipping\"\n\n  lint:\n    name: Lint\n    needs: changes\n    if: needs.changes.outputs.src == 'true'\n    runs-on: ubuntu-latest\n    steps:\n      - name: Checkout code\n        uses: actions/checkout@v6\n\n      - name: Setup Go\n        uses: actions/setup-go@v6\n        with:\n          go-version: '1.26'\n          cache: true\n\n      - name: Run golangci-lint\n        uses: golangci/golangci-lint-action@v9\n        with:\n          version: v2.11.4\n\n  lint-skip:\n    name: Lint\n    needs: changes\n    if: needs.changes.outputs.src != 'true'\n    runs-on: ubuntu-latest\n    steps:\n      - run: echo \"No Go changes, skipping\"\n```\n\n- Step 3: Commit\n```bash\ngit add .github/workflows/go.yml\ngit commit -m \"ci: refactor go.yml — always-run with paths-filter bypass\"\n```\n\n## Context\n\nWorking directory: /Users/jobinlawrance/Project/raven\nCurrent branch: ci/playwright-mypy-workflows\n\nThis is one of four parallel workflow refactors (go.yml, python.yml, frontend.yml, docker.yml). All four will be committed to this branch and then pushed as a single PR to main in a subsequent task.\n\nThe goal: make Go CI always run on all PRs (not just when Go files change), using skip jobs to satisfy GitHub branch protection checks even when no Go files changed.\n\nImportant: The `changes` detector job uses `actions/checkout@v4`. The real jobs use `actions/checkout@v6` and `actions/setup-go@v6` — these match the existing workflow versions already passing CI.\n\n## Your Job\n\n1. Write the file exactly as specified\n2. Commit\n3. Report back\n\n## Report Format\n\n- **Status:** DONE | DONE_WITH_CONCERNS | BLOCKED | NEEDS_CONTEXT\n- Files changed\n- Commit SHA",

{
  "description": "Implement Task 4: Refactor docker.yml",
  "prompt": "You are implementing Task 4: Refactor docker.yml — path-filter bypass\n\n## Task Description\n\n**Files:**\n- Modify: `.github/workflows/docker.yml`\n\nDocker uses a matrix job (`Build ${{ matrix.image }}`). Skip jobs must have explicit names matching the expanded check strings (`Build go-api`, `Build python-worker`).\n\nReplace the entire contents of `.github/workflows/docker.yml` with:\n\n```yaml\nname: Docker Build & Push\n\non:\n  push:\n    branches: [main]\n  pull_request:\n    branches: [main]\n\npermissions:\n  contents: read\n  packages: write\n\njobs:\n  changes:\n    runs-on: ubuntu-latest\n    outputs:\n      src: ${{ steps.filter.outputs.src }}\n    steps:\n      - uses: actions/checkout@v4\n      - uses: dorny/paths-filter@v4\n        id: filter\n        with:\n          filters: |\n            src:\n              - 'Dockerfile'\n              - 'ai-worker/Dockerfile'\n              - 'docker-compose.yml'\n              - '.github/workflows/docker.yml'\n\n  build:\n    name: Build ${{ matrix.image }}\n    needs: changes\n    if: needs.changes.outputs.src == 'true'\n    runs-on: ubuntu-latest\n    strategy:\n      fail-fast: false\n      matrix:\n        include:\n          - image: go-api\n            context: .\n            dockerfile: Dockerfile\n          - image: python-worker\n            context: ai-worker\n            dockerfile: ai-worker/Dockerfile\n    steps:\n      - name: Checkout code\n        uses: actions/checkout@v6\n\n      - name: Set up QEMU\n        uses: docker/setup-qemu-action@v4\n\n      - name: Set up Docker Buildx\n        uses: docker/setup-buildx-action@v4\n\n      - name: Login to GHCR\n        if: github.event_name == 'push' && github.ref == 'refs/heads/main'\n        uses: docker/login-action@v4\n        with:\n          registry: ghcr.io\n          username: ${{ github.actor }}\n          password: ${{ secrets.GITHUB_TOKEN }}\n\n      - name: Build ${{ matrix.image }}\n        uses: docker/build-push-action@v7\n        with:\n          context: ${{ matrix.context }}\n          file: ${{ matrix.dockerfile }}\n          push: ${{ github.event_name == 'push' && github.ref == 'refs/heads/main' }}\n          platforms: linux/amd64,linux/arm64\n          cache-from: type=gha\n          cache-to: type=gha,mode=max\n          tags: ghcr.io/${{ github.repository_owner }}/${{ matrix.image }}:${{ github.sha }}\n\n  build-skip-go-api:\n    name: Build go-api\n    needs: changes\n    if: needs.changes.outputs.src != 'true'\n    runs-on: ubuntu-latest\n    steps:\n      - run: echo \"No Docker changes, skipping\"\n\n  build-skip-python-worker:\n    name: Build python-worker\n    needs: changes\n    if: needs.changes.outputs.src != 'true'\n    runs-on: ubuntu-latest\n    steps:\n      - run: echo \"No Docker changes, skipping\"\n```\n\nThen commit:\n```bash\ngit add .github/workflows/docker.yml\ngit commit -m \"ci: refactor docker.yml — always-run with paths-filter bypass\"\n```\n\n## Context\n\nWorking directory: /Users/jobinlawrance/Project/raven\nCurrent branch: ci/playwright-mypy-workflows\n\nThis is one of four parallel workflow refactors. The `changes` detector uses `actions/checkout@v4`. The real build job uses `actions/checkout@v6` — matching the existing workflow version.\n\nThe two skip jobs (`build-skip-go-api`, `build-skip-python-worker`) have explicit `name:` fields `Build go-api` and `Build python-worker` — these match the matrix-expanded check strings that GitHub branch protection will require.\n\n## Your Job\n\n1. Write the file exactly as specified\n2. Commit\n3. Report back\n\n## Report Format\n\n- **Status:** DONE | DONE_WITH_CONCERNS | BLOCKED | NEEDS_CONTEXT\n- Files changed\n- Commit SHA",

I now have a complete picture. Here is the full review.

---

## Code Review: CI Workflow Path-Filter Refactor (Tasks 1-4)

### What Was Reviewed

Four workflow files refactored from workflow-level `paths:` triggers to always-run workflows with `dorny/paths-filter@v4` skip-job pattern, across commits `46ebb94` through `6c3b7f6`.

---

### Strengths

The core pattern is implemented correctly and consistently across all four files:

- `dorny/paths-filter@v4` is used in every `changes` job — not v3.
- All `changes` jobs correctly wire the `outputs: src: ${{ steps.filter.outputs.src }}` pattern.
- Every real job has `needs: changes` and `if: needs.changes.outputs.src == 'true'`.
- Every skip job has `needs: changes` and `if: needs.changes.outputs.src != 'true'`.
- The `==` vs `!=` split is applied consistently — there are no jobs missing either side of the guard.
- All four `changes` jobs use `actions/checkout@v4` (the correct pinned version for a pure filter step).
- YAML syntax is valid in all four files.
- The docker matrix skip pattern is handled correctly: two separate skip jobs with `name: Build go-api` and `name: Build python-worker` match the expanded names of the matrix real job (`name: Build ${{ matrix.image }}`). Branch protection will see identical check names regardless of which path runs.

---

### Issues

#### Critical

**None found.** Skip job display names match real job display names in all seven job pairs across all four files.

#### Important

**1. `python.yml` — filter adds `.github/workflows/python.yml` that was absent from original trigger (Minor scope creep, but functionally correct)**

The original `python.yml` trigger paths were only `ai-worker/**` and `proto/**`. The new filter adds `.github/workflows/python.yml` as a third pattern. This is a silent behavioral change: previously, editing `python.yml` itself did not re-run CI; now it will.

File: `/Users/jobinlawrance/Project/raven/.github/workflows/python.yml`, lines 22-26.

This is arguably the right behavior (go, frontend, and docker all already included their own workflow file in their trigger lists), and it makes the four files consistent. However, it is a deviation from the original trigger spec that was not present in the base. If the plan explicitly said "preserve existing paths exactly," this needs acknowledgment. If the plan said "make them consistent," it is correct.

Action required: Confirm whether this addition was intentional. If yes, it is a good consistency fix. If not, remove `.github/workflows/python.yml` from the filter.

**2. Pre-existing `actions/checkout@v6` and `actions/setup-*@v6` — non-existent versions inherited unchanged**

This is pre-existing from the base SHA, not introduced by this refactor, but it is worth flagging. The `changes` jobs correctly use `actions/checkout@v4`, but all real jobs use `actions/checkout@v6`, `actions/setup-go@v6`, `actions/setup-node@v6`, and `actions/setup-python@v6`. These are not valid released versions as of this writing — latest stable for each is v4. Using non-existent version tags will cause GitHub Actions to fall back to the latest commit on the tag branch or fail depending on how the action is published.

This refactor did not introduce these versions, so they are out of scope for Tasks 1-4. They should be addressed in a separate cleanup ticket. All four workflow files are affected in their real job steps.

#### Minor

**3. `changes` job has no display `name:` field**

All four `changes` jobs lack a `name:` key. In the GitHub Actions UI this shows up as the raw job ID `changes`. This is not functional but slightly reduces readability in the Actions tab. Consistent with the other skip jobs all having names, adding `name: Detect changes` would be clean.

This is a suggestion only — it has no impact on branch protection or correctness.

---

### Checklist Verification

| Check | Result |
|---|---|
| `dorny/paths-filter@v4` used (not v3) | Pass — all four files |
| `needs: changes` on all non-changes jobs | Pass — all 9 real/skip jobs |
| `if: == 'true'` on real jobs | Pass |
| `if: != 'true'` on skip jobs | Pass |
| Skip job `name:` matches real job `name:` | Pass — all 7 pairs |
| Matrix skip names match expanded matrix names | Pass — go-api and python-worker |
| Workflow-level `paths:` removed from `on:` | Pass — all four files |
| YAML syntax valid | Pass — all four files |
| Path filter lists match original trigger paths | Pass for go, frontend, docker; Minor deviation in python (workflow file added) |
| Unnecessary additions | One: the `.github/workflows/python.yml` path in python.yml filter |

---

### Assessment

**Approved with one clarification needed.**

The implementation is structurally sound and the branch protection skip-job pattern is correctly implemented across all four workflows. The only item requiring a decision is the `.github/workflows/python.yml` addition to the python filter — confirm whether it was intentional. If so, no changes are needed. If not, a one-line removal from `/Users/jobinlawrance/Project/raven/.github/workflows/python.yml` line 26 resolves it.

The pre-existing `@v6` action versions are a separate concern that should be tracked independently of this refactor.

```
I now have everything needed. Here is the review.

---

## Task 9 — `.github/workflows/claude.yml`: `pull-requests: write`

**Approved.**

The permission upgrade is correct and necessary. The `anthropics/claude-code-action@v1` action needs `pull-requests: write` to perform its core functions: posting review comments in response to PR review comment triggers, posting inline code suggestions, and updating PR descriptions. Without it the action can read PR state but silently fails to write back, which is the root cause of the original bug.

One observation worth noting: `actions: read` is declared twice — once in the `permissions` block (line 26) and again in the `additional_permissions` input (lines 40-42). The second declaration is redundant. It is not harmful, but it is noise. This is a pre-existing issue, not introduced by this change, and is low priority.

Security assessment: the scope is appropriate. The workflow only triggers when `@claude` is explicitly mentioned in a comment or issue body, which limits the attack surface. `contents` remains `read`-only, so the action cannot push commits directly. `pull-requests: write` is the minimum required scope for a review-comment bot. The comparison with `opencode.yml` (which stays at `read`) is expected — opencode does not write comments back.

---

## Task 10 — `CLAUDE.md`

**Approved with one minor suggestion.**

The content is accurate, actionable, and correctly encodes the project conventions documented in memory (squash-merge only, no `--no-verify`, no direct pushes to `main`).

One gap worth addressing: the file says "Do not wait for CI — enqueue immediately after creation" but does not say what to do if `gh pr merge --auto --squash` fails because branch protection rules require the PR to have at least one approval before auto-merge can be enabled. On this repo that scenario could arise, and an agent following these instructions to the letter would silently leave the PR without auto-merge queued. A one-line clarification — e.g. "If auto-merge is rejected due to required reviews, leave the PR open; a human reviewer will approve and the auto-merge queue will activate" — would prevent an agent from either retrying in a loop or mistakenly force-merging.

Everything else — the branch naming table, the three rules, the `--squash` flag — is correct and complete.

Is the YAML valid and well-formed?\n2. Are the Mergify conditions using correct v2 syntax?\n3. Is `update: {}` (not `rebase: {}`) used for the auto-update rule?\n4. Any ordering issue between the three rules?\n5. Any condition that could cause unintended merges?\n\nReturn: Approved or specific issues only."
}
```

> TOOL

tool_result
id: toolu_01MaqxJZXUoQvq7MJeGgrAsD
```
I now have everything needed for the review. Here is the analysis:

---

**YAML validity — Pass.** Python's `yaml.safe_load` parses the file without error. Indentation and quoting are consistent throughout.

**Mergify v2 syntax — Pass.** All conditions use the current attribute-operator-value form (`base = main`, `-draft`, `check-success = "..."`, `label != "needs-review"`, `author = dependabot[bot]`). No deprecated `label = ""` shorthand or v1 `conditions:` quirks are present.

**`update: {}` vs `rebase: {}` — Pass.** Rule 1 correctly uses `update: {}`, which is the current Mergify action for rebasing a PR onto its base branch. The deprecated `rebase: {}` action is not used.

**Rule ordering — Pass.** The ordering is safe. Rules in Mergify `pull_request_rules` are evaluated independently (not as a pipeline), so order has no semantic effect on outcomes. The chosen order (update, then merge, then flag) is logically readable and fine.

**Conditions that could cause unintended merges — one issue found.**

The auto-squash-merge rule does not include `-draft` as the only protection against non-ready PRs. It does guard against `draft` and `WIP` titles, but the Docker check string is constructed from a matrix job. The GitHub check name for a matrix job is `"Docker Build & Push / Build go-api"` — which concatenates the workflow name (`Docker Build & Push`), a slash, and the job `name:` field (`Build ${{ matrix.image }}`). The job `name` evaluates to `Build go-api` and `Build python-worker`. Let me verify those resolve correctly.

The `.mergify.yml` references:
```
check-success = "Docker Build & Push / Build go-api"
check-success = "Docker Build & Push / Build python-worker"
```

The workflow name is `Docker Build & Push` and the job `name:` is `Build ${{ matrix.image }}` with `matrix.image` values of `go-api` and `python-worker`. GitHub resolves these at runtime to `Build go-api` and `Build python-worker`, so the combined check names will be exactly `Docker Build & Push / Build go-api` and `Docker Build & Push / Build python-worker`. The strings match. Pass.

One genuine concern remains:

**Important — Missing `approved-reviews-by` or `#approved-reviews-by >= 1` condition in the auto-merge rule.**

The auto-squash-merge rule gates on all CI checks passing and `label != "needs-review"`, but it does not require any human approval. This means any PR (not by Dependabot, not labelled `needs-review`, not a draft, not WIP) that passes all CI jobs will be automatically merged without a single human review. This contradicts the project's own `needs-review` label pattern — the label is only added retroactively by the Dependabot major-bump rule. A non-Dependabot PR with a major breaking change has no automatic protection; it will auto-merge as soon as CI is green.

Recommended fix — add one condition to the merge rule:

```yaml
- "#approved-reviews-by >= 1"
```

This requires at least one GitHub-approved review before Mergify can merge, while still allowing the automation to handle the actual merge action once approval is granted. Alternatively, if branch protection already enforces required reviews, this is covered at the GitHub level and the Mergify condition is redundant but harmless to add for defence-in-depth.

**Minor — no `label` cleanup in the merge action.**

If a PR somehow acquires the `needs-review` label and it is later removed manually to allow merging, any other informational labels (e.g., stale) are left untouched. Not a blocking issue but worth noting if label hygiene matters later.

---

**Summary**

| Check | Result |
|---|---|
| YAML valid and well-formed | Pass |
| Mergify v2 condition syntax | Pass |
| `update: {}` (not `rebase: {}`) | Pass |
| Rule ordering | Pass |
| Check strings match actual workflow job names | Pass |
| Risk of unintended merge without human review | Issue — Important |

The file is functionally correct and will work. The one issue to address before relying on this in production: add `"#approved-reviews-by >= 1"` to the auto-squash-merge rule's conditions, or confirm that GitHub branch protection already enforces required reviewers (making Mergify respect that gate automatically).

name: Frontend CI

on:
  push:
    branches: [main]
    paths:
      - 'frontend/**'
      - '.github/workflows/frontend.yml'
  pull_request:
    branches: [main]
    paths:
      - 'frontend/**'
      - '.github/workflows/frontend.yml'

permissions:
  contents: read

jobs:
  build:
    name: Lint & Build
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    defaults:
      run:
        working-directory: frontend
    steps:
      - name: Checkout code
        uses: actions/checkout@v6

      - name: Setup Node.js
        uses: actions/setup-node@v6
        with:
          node-version: '22'
          cache: npm
          cache-dependency-path: frontend/package-lock.json

      - name: Install dependencies
        run: npm ci

      - name: Lint
        run: npm run lint

      - name: Type check
        run: npx vue-tsc --noEmit

      - name: Build
        run: npm run build

  e2e:
    name: Playwright E2E
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    defaults:
      run:
        working-directory: frontend
    steps:
      - name: Checkout code
        uses: actions/checkout@v6

      - name: Setup Node.js
        uses: actions/setup-node@v6
        with:
          node-version: '22'
          cache: npm
          cache-dependency-path: frontend/package-lock.json

      - name: Install dependencies
        run: npm ci

      - name: Build
        run: npm run build

      - name: Install Playwright browsers
        run: npx playwright install --with-deps chromium

      - name: Run Playwright tests
        run: npm run test:e2e
